<DIV style="margin-top: 18pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">W-H
    ENERGY SERVICES, INC.<BR>
    </FONT></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">NOTES&#160;TO
    CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL
    STATEMENTS&#160;&#151;&#160;(Continued)</FONT></B>
</DIV>
<DIV style="margin-top: 18pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    change resulted in adjustments that decreased cost of revenues
    by $0.8&#160;million and $1.0&#160;million, increased net income
    by $0.6&#160;million and $0.7&#160;million, and increased
    diluted earnings per share by $0.02 and $0.02, respectively, as
    compared to amounts previously reported.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Research
    and Development</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    Research and development costs primarily represent salaries of
    research personnel and their related expenditures. Such
    activities are expensed when incurred. For the years ended
    December&#160;31, 2007, 2006 and 2005, research and development
    costs were $21.4&#160;million, $17.2&#160;million and
    $16.3&#160;million, respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Income
    Taxes</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> utilizes
    the liability method of accounting for income taxes. Under the
    liability method, deferred tax assets and liabilities are
    recognized for the future tax consequences attributable to
    differences between the financial statement carrying values of
    existing assets and liabilities and their respective tax bases
    based on enacted tax rates.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    recognizes liabilities for anticipated tax issues based on its
    estimate of whether, and the extent to which, additional taxes
    will be due. These liabilities are adjusted accordingly as
    information on the associated tax issues becomes available.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    In June 2006, the Financial Accounting Standards Board
    (&#147;FASB&#148;) issued FASB Interpretation No.&#160;48,
    <I>&#147;Accounting for Uncertainty in Income Taxes&#148;
    </I>(&#147;FIN&#160;48&#148;). FIN&#160;48 clarifies the
    application of SFAS&#160;No.&#160;109, &#147;<I>Accounting for
    Income Taxes,&#148; </I>by establishing a threshold condition
    that a tax position must meet for any part of the benefit of
    that position to be recognized in the financial statements. In
    addition to recognition, FIN&#160;48 provides guidance
    concerning measurement, recognition, classification and
    disclosure of tax positions. FIN&#160;48 is effective for fiscal
    years beginning after December&#160;15, 2006; accordingly,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> adopted
    FIN&#160;48 effective as of January&#160;1, 2007. Upon adoption,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    recognized a cumulative effect adjustment of approximately
    $4.1&#160;million as a reduction to retained earnings to
    increase the liability for unrecognized tax benefits. See
    Note&#160;9 for more information.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    In May 2007, the FASB issued FSP
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">FIN&#160;48-1,</FONT>
    an amendment to FIN&#160;48, which provides guidance on how an
    entity is to determine whether a tax position has effectively
    settled for purposes of recognizing previously unrecognized tax
    benefits. Specifically, this guidance states that an entity
    would recognize a benefit when a tax position is effectively
    settled using the following criteria: (1)&#160;the taxing
    authority has completed its examination including all appeals
    and administrative reviews; (2)&#160;the entity does not plan to
    appeal or litigate any aspect of the tax position; and
    (3)&#160;it is remote that the taxing authority would examine or
    reexamine any aspect of the tax position, assuming the taxing
    authority has full knowledge of all relevant information
    relative to making their assessment on the position.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> applied
    this guidance in its adoption of FIN&#160;48 and it will
    continue to apply this guidance as applicable in future periods.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Use of
    Estimates and Assumptions</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    The preparation of financial statements in conformity with
    accounting principles generally accepted in the United States
    requires management to make estimates and assumptions that
    affect the reported amounts of assets and liabilities, the
    disclosure of contingent assets and liabilities at the date of
    the financial statements and the reported amounts of revenues
    and expenses during the reporting period. Actual results could
    differ from those estimates.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Financial
    Instruments</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    considers the fair value of all financial instruments (primarily
    long-term debt) not to be materially different from their
    carrying values at the end of each fiscal year based on
    management&#146;s estimate of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    ability to borrow funds under terms and conditions similar to
    those of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    existing debt.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <BR>
    F-11
</DIV><!-- END PAGE WIDTH -->
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->

<DIV style="margin-top: 18pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">W-H
    ENERGY SERVICES, INC.<BR>
    </FONT></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">NOTES&#160;TO
    CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL
    STATEMENTS&#160;&#151;&#160;(Continued)</FONT></B>
</DIV>
<DIV style="margin-top: 18pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    On May&#160;18, 2005,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> entered
    into interest rate swap agreements with a total notional amount
    of $150.0&#160;million related to its credit facility. The
    interest rate swap agreements have been designated as and
    qualify as cash flow hedging instruments. The interest rate swap
    agreements are fully effective, and have resulted in no gain or
    loss due to ineffectiveness being recorded in net income in the
    Consolidated Statement of Operations and Comprehensive Income.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> records
    the fair values of the interest rate swap agreements on its
    Consolidated Balance Sheet. See Note&#160;7 to the Consolidated
    Financial Statements for more information.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    With the exception of the operating leases on real property and
    automobile leases discussed in Note&#160;8 to the Consolidated
    Financial Statements,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> has no
    off-balance sheet debt or other off-balance sheet financing
    arrangements.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Accounting
    for Share-Based Compensation</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> expenses
    the grant-date fair value of stock options and other
    equity-based compensation issued to employees. For awards with
    service conditions and a graded vesting schedule only,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    recognizes compensation expense on a straight-line basis over
    the requisite service period for the entire award.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    policy is to issue new shares of common stock to satisfy stock
    option exercises or grants of restricted shares.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    The fair value of option awards will be estimated on the date of
    grant using a binomial lattice-based option valuation model,
    which incorporates ranges of assumptions for inputs. The
    assumptions will be determined as follows:
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0">

<TR>
    <TD width="4%"></TD>
    <TD width="2%"></TD>
    <TD width="94%"></TD>
</TR>

<TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>    &#149;&#160;
</TD>
    <TD align="left">
    the expected volatility will be a blend of implied volatility
    based on market-traded options on
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    common stock and historical volatility of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    stock over the contractual life of the options;
</TD>
</TR>


<TR style="line-height: 6pt; font-size: 1pt"><TD>&nbsp;</TD></TR>


<TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>    &#149;&#160;
</TD>
    <TD align="left">
    historical data will be used to estimate option exercise and
    employee termination behavior within the valuation model;
    separate groups of employees that have similar historical
    exercise behavior are considered separately for valuation
    purposes. The expected life of options granted will be derived
    from the output of the option valuation model and represents the
    period of time the options are expected to be
    outstanding;&#160;and
</TD>
</TR>


<TR style="line-height: 6pt; font-size: 1pt"><TD>&nbsp;</TD></TR>


<TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>    &#149;&#160;
</TD>
    <TD align="left">
    the risk-free interest rate will be based on the
    U.S.&#160;Treasury yield curve in effect at the time of grant
    for periods within the contractual life of the option.
</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    Through December&#160;31, 2005,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    accounted for all stock-based employee compensation plans under
    the recognition and measurement provisions of APB Opinion
    No.&#160;25, &#147;<I>Accounting for Stock Issued to
    Employees</I>,&#148; and related interpretations. Under APB
    No.&#160;25, no stock-based employee costs are reflected in net
    income, as all options granted under those plans had an exercise
    price equal to or in excess of the market value of the
    underlying common stock on the date of grant.
</DIV>

    <BR>
    F-12
</DIV><!-- END PAGE WIDTH -->

</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Foreign
    Currency Translations</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    The operations of foreign locations were translated into
    U.S.&#160;dollars based on the current exchange rates at the
    respective balance sheet dates and the weighted-average rates
    during each year for the statements of operations and
    comprehensive income. For the years ended December&#160;31,
    2007, 2006 and 2005, the translation adjustments were a loss of
    $0.1&#160;million, a gain of $1.6&#160;million, and a loss of
    $1.7&#160;million, respectively, and are reflected as foreign
    currency translation adjustments in the consolidated statements
    of operations and comprehensive income.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Earnings
    per Share</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    Basic earnings per share is computed by dividing net income by
    the weighted average number of shares of common stock
    outstanding for the period. Diluted earnings per share is
    computed by dividing net income by the weighted average number
    of shares of common stock outstanding for the period as adjusted
    for the dilutive effect of stock options and restricted shares.
    For the years ended December&#160;31, 2007 and 2006, there were
    no anti-dilutive stock options, therefore, the effect of all
    stock options were included in the diluted earnings per share
    calculation. For the year ended December&#160;31, 2005,
    2,382&#160;shares were excluded from the calculation because the
    exercise price of the underlying stock options exceeded the
    average price of our common stock for the applicable period.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <BR>
    F-13
</DIV><!-- END PAGE WIDTH -->

</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Recent
    Accounting Pronouncements</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    In September 2006, the FASB issued SFAS&#160;No.&#160;157,
    <I>&#147;Fair Value Measurements&#148;</I>
    (&#147;SFAS&#160;No.&#160;157&#148;), which is intended to
    increase consistency and comparability in fair value
    measurements in financial statements by defining fair value,
    establishing a framework for measuring fair value and expanding
    disclosures about fair value measurements.
    SFAS&#160;No.&#160;157 is effective for financial statements
    issued for fiscal years beginning after November&#160;15, 2007,
    and interim periods within those fiscal years.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> will
    adopt SFAS&#160;No.&#160;157 on January&#160;1, 2008 and does
    not anticipate it will have a material impact on its
    Consolidated Financial Statements. FSP
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">FAS&#160;157-2,</FONT>
    <I>&#147;Effective Date of FASB Statement
    No.&#160;157,&#148;</I> provides a one-year deferral of the
    effective date of FASB Statement 157, <I>Fair Value
    Measurements, </I>for nonfinancial assets and nonfinancial
    liabilities, except those that are recognized or disclosed in
    financial statements at fair value on a recurring basis.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    In February 2007, the FASB issued SFAS&#160;No.&#160;159,
    <I>&#147;The Fair Value Option for Financial Assets and
    Financial Liabilities (as amended)&#148;
    </I>(&#147;SFAS&#160;No.&#160;159&#148;), which permits entities
    to choose to measure most financial instruments and certain
    other items at fair value. SFAS&#160;No.&#160;159 is effective
    for financial statements issued for fiscal years beginning after
    November&#160;15, 2007, and interim periods within those fiscal
    years. <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    will adopt SFAS&#160;No.&#160;159 on January&#160;1, 2008 and
    does not anticipate it will have a material impact on its
    Consolidated Financial Statements.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    In December 2007, the FASB issued SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141
    (revised 2007), <I>&#147;Business Combinations&#148;
    </I>(&#147;SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141R&#148;) to change how an
    entity accounts for the acquisition of a business.
    SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141R carries forward the existing
    requirements to account for all business combinations using the
    acquisition method (formerly called the purchase method). In
    general, SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141R will require acquisition-date
    fair value measurement of identifiable assets acquired,
    liabilities assumed and non-controlling interests in the
    acquiree. SFAS&#160;141R will eliminate the current
    cost&#160;&#151; based purchase method under
    SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141. The new measurement requirements will
    result in the recognition of the full amount of acquisition-date
    goodwill, which includes amounts attributable to non-controlling
    interests. The acquirer will recognize in income any gain or
    loss on the remeasurement to acquisition-date fair value of
    consideration transferred or of previously acquired equity
    interests in the acquiree. The direct costs incurred to effect a
    business combination and the costs the acquirer expects to incur
    under a plan to restructure an acquired business will be charged
    to expense when incurred. SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141R will also
    change the accounting for contingent consideration, in process
    research and development, contingencies, and restructuring
    costs. In addition, changes in deferred tax asset valuation
    allowances and acquired income tax uncertainties in a business
    combination that occur after the measurement period will impact
    income taxes under SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141R.
    SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141R is effective for fiscal years beginning
    on or after December&#160;15, 2008 and interim periods within
    those years.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> intends
    to adopt SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141R effective January&#160;1, 2009
    and apply its provisions prospectively.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    currently does not believe that the adoption of Statement
    No.&#160;141R will have a significant effect on its financial
    statements; however, the effect is dependent upon whether
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> makes
    any future acquisitions and the specific terms of those
    acquisitions.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141R amends the goodwill impairment test
    requirements in SFAS&#160;No.&#160;142. For a goodwill
    impairment test as of a date after the effective date of
    SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141R, the value of the reporting unit and the
    amount of implied goodwill, calculated in the second step of the
    test, will be determined in accordance with the measurement and
    recognition guidance on accounting for business combinations
    under SFAS&#160;No.&#160;141R. This
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <BR>
    F-14

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    On January&#160;18, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> acquired
    a distributor of drilling fluids products for cash consideration
    of approximately $4.9&#160;million. On February&#160;15, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> acquired
    a directional drilling company for total consideration of
    approximately $14.7&#160;million including cash of
    $10.8&#160;million and 90,556&#160;shares of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> common
    stock.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    In 2006, <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    acquired Mt. Pulaski Products, Inc., including its related
    companies (&#147;Mt. Pulaski&#148;). Mt. Pulaski supplies
    products used in various industrial applications including
    drilling fluids. Total consideration for this acquisition of
    approximately $10.4&#160;million included $9.4&#160;million in
    cash and 20,358&#160;shares of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> common
    stock.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    In 2005, <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    acquired Madden Systems, Inc. (&#147;Madden&#148;) to enhance
    the technology of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    operating companies that offer wireline logging services. Total
    consideration for this acquisition of $3.5&#160;million included
    $2.5&#160;million in cash and 31,422&#160;shares of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> common
    stock.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    Unaudited proforma consolidated financial information for these
    acquisitions have not been included as the results were not
    material to current operations.

<TR valign="top">
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">6.&#160;&#160;</FONT></B>
</TD>
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Credit
    Facility:</FONT></B>
</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    maintains a revolving credit facility with certain lenders to
    provide for its cash, liquidity and other borrowing needs. The
    credit facility provides for aggregate borrowings of up to
    $375.0&#160;million and matures on May&#160;5, 2010. As of
    December&#160;31, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> had an
    outstanding loan balance of $150.0&#160;million and
    approximately $10.6&#160;million in letters of credit issued
    under its credit facility, resulting in an available borrowing
    capacity on such date of approximately $214.4&#160;million.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    Amounts borrowed under the credit facility bear interest, at
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    election, at either a variable rate equal to LIBOR, plus a
    margin ranging from 1.0% to 2.0%, depending upon
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    leverage ratio, or an alternate base rate equal to the higher of
    (1)&#160;the prime rate or (2)&#160;the federal funds rate plus
    0.5%, plus a margin ranging from zero to 1.0%, depending upon
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    leverage ratio. Including the effect of the interest rate swap
    agreements described in Note&#160;7,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> was
    incurring interest at a weighted average rate of approximately
    5.2% on its total outstanding borrowings as of December&#160;31,
    2007.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    The credit facility is secured by a lien on substantially all of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    property and assets, a pledge of all the capital stock of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    domestic subsidiaries and a pledge of not greater than 65% of
    the capital stock of each of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    top tier foreign subsidiaries. In addition, the credit facility
    is guaranteed by all of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    domestic subsidiaries. The credit facility requires, among other
    things, that
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> maintain
    certain financial ratios, including a leverage ratio and an
    interest coverage ratio, and a specified net worth. The credit
    facility limits the amount of capital expenditures
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> may
    make, the amount of debt
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> may
    incur outside of the credit facility, the amount of future
    investments
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> may
    make, the ability of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> to pay
    dividends and the ability of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> to
    engage in certain business combination transactions. As of
    December&#160;31, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> is in
    compliance with the financial ratios and other limitations of
    the credit facility.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <BR>
    F-17
</DIV><!-- END PAGE WIDTH -->

    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">7.&#160;&#160;</FONT></B>
</TD>
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Interest
    Rate Swap Agreements</FONT></B>
</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    On May&#160;18, 2005,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> entered
    into interest rate swap agreements with a total notional amount
    of $150.0&#160;million related to its credit facility. Under
    these agreements,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> receives
    interest at a floating rate equal to three month LIBOR and pays
    interest at a fixed rate of 4.24%. The interest rate swap
    agreements have been designated as and qualify as cash flow
    hedging instruments. The interest rate swap agreements are fully
    effective, and have resulted in no gain or loss due to
    ineffectiveness being recorded in net income in the Consolidated
    Statement of Operations and Comprehensive Income. For the years
    ended December&#160;31, 2007, 2006 and 2005,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    recognized net gains of $1.7&#160;million and $1.2&#160;million,
    and net losses of $0.5&#160;million, respectively, to interest
    expense resulting from the impact of the swap agreements.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> has
    recorded the fair value of the interest rate swap agreements on
    its Consolidated Balance Sheet, which was in aggregate a
    liability of $1.5&#160;million at December&#160;31, 2007 and an
    asset of $3.7&#160;million at December&#160;31, 2006 based on
    the fair value of the instruments. As of December&#160;31, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    anticipates that approximately $0.1&#160;million of the
    liability will be recognized as losses resulting in additions to
    interest expense during 2008.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Employment
    Agreements</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> has
    entered into employment agreements with its corporate officers.
    Under these agreements, each officer receives a set base salary,
    subject to adjustment, an annual discretionary bonus based on
    specific objectives to be determined by the compensation
    committee, an automobile allowance and certain fringe benefits
    as may be available to such executive officers. The agreements
    are generally for original terms of two to three years, with
    certain automatic renewal provisions. The agreements also
    contain a termination clause, which requires a two-year payment
    (2.5&#160;years in the case of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    Chief Executive Officer) based on the officer&#146;s salary and
    historical bonus amounts received, in the event of termination
    without cause or certain change of control events.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> also has
    employment agreements with certain non-corporate officers and
    other key employees. The agreements are for original terms of
    two to three years and provide for severance pay upon the
    occurrence of certain events including involuntary termination.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Litigation</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> is from
    time to time a party or otherwise subject to legal proceedings,
    claims, investigations and other proceedings in the ordinary
    course of its business. These matters typically involve tort,
    workers compensation, employment, commercial and infringement
    and other intellectual property claims. Where appropriate,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> makes
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <BR>
    F-18

    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">W-H

    </FONT></B>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    provision for a liability with respect to these claims in its
    financial statements in accordance with generally accepted
    accounting principles. These provisions are reviewed
    periodically and adjusted to reflect the impact of negotiations,
    settlements, rulings, advice of legal counsel and events
    pertaining to a particular case. Litigation is inherently
    unpredictable. Based upon information currently available,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> believes
    that its ultimate liability with respect to these proceedings
    and claims will not materially affect its consolidated results
    of operations or financial position.
</DIV>

    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">9.&#160;&#160;</FONT></B>

    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Income
    Taxes</FONT></B>
</TD>
</TR>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    U.S.&#160;deferred income taxes and related foreign dividend
    withholding taxes have not been provided on the undistributed
    earnings and profits of the company&#146;s foreign subsidiaries.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    considers such earnings to be permanently reinvested outside the
    United States. It is not practicable to estimate the amount of
    deferred income taxes associated with these unremitted earnings.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    As of December&#160;31, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> had
    deferred tax assets of $4.3&#160;million relating to
    $1.3&#160;million of state net operating loss (&#147;NOL&#148;)
    carry-forwards and $3.0&#160;million of foreign NOL
    carry-forwards. The foreign NOL&#146;s have no expiration date.
    State NOL carry-forwards expire beginning in 2008 until 2021.
    The NOL and credit carry-forwards can be used to reduce
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    federal, state and foreign income taxes payable in future years.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    ability to realize the entire benefit of its deferred tax assets
    requires that
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> achieve
    certain future earnings levels prior to the expiration of its
    NOL carry-forwards.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    Valuation allowances have been established for uncertainties in
    realizing the benefit of tax loss and credit carry-forwards.
    While <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    expects to realize the net deferred tax assets, changes in
    future taxable income or in tax laws may alter this expectation.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> could be
    required to record an additional valuation allowance against
    certain or all of its remaining deferred tax assets if market
    conditions deteriorate or future earnings are below current
    estimates. As of December&#160;31, 2007, the valuation allowance
    relates principally to foreign NOL&#146;s. The valuation
    allowance decreased approximately $1.5&#160;million in 2007,
    increased $1.4&#160;million in 2006 and increased
    $0.3&#160;million in 2005. The change related principally to
    amounts provided on foreign NOL&#146;s in jurisdictions where
    management does not believe
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> will be
    able to utilize the losses in future periods.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> is
    subject to periodic audits by the Internal Revenue Service and
    other foreign, state and local taxing authorities. Currently,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> is
    involved in various federal, state and foreign income tax
    examinations by tax authorities for years ranging from 2000
    through 2005. These audits may challenge certain of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    tax positions such as the timing and amount of deductions and
    allocation of taxable income to the various tax jurisdictions.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <BR>
    F-20
</DIV><!-- END PAGE WIDTH -->

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    Income tax contingencies are accounted for in accordance with
    the provisions FIN&#160;48, and may require significant
    management judgment in estimating final outcomes. Actual results
    could materially differ from these estimates and could
    significantly affect the effective tax rate and cash flows in
    future years.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    Prior to adoption of FIN&#160;48,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    recognized liabilities for anticipated tax issues based on its
    estimate of whether, and the extent to which, additional taxes
    will be due. These liabilities were adjusted accordingly as
    information on the associated tax issues becomes available. As
    of December&#160;31, 2006, amounts reserved for such
    contingencies were $6.2&#160;million.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> adopted
    the provisions of FIN&#160;48 effective as of January&#160;1,
    2007. Upon adoption,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    recognized a cumulative effect adjustment of approximately
    $4.1&#160;million as a reduction to retained earnings to
    increase the liability for unrecognized tax benefits. A
    reconciliation of the beginning and ending amount of the
    unrecognized tax benefits is as follows (in thousands):
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    As of December&#160;31, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> had
    unrecognized tax benefits of $12.5&#160;million, of which
    $7.4&#160;million would have an impact on the annual effective
    tax rate upon recognition. Of the remaining balance,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> had
    $2.9&#160;million of various tax positions for which the
    ultimate deductibility is highly certain but for which there is
    uncertainty about the timing of such deductibility. Because of
    the impact of deferred tax accounting, other than interest and
    penalties, the disallowance of the shorter deductibility period
    would not affect the annual effective tax rate but would
    accelerate the payment of cash to the taxing authority to an
    earlier period.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    recognizes interest and penalties related to unrecognized tax
    benefits in income tax expense. This is an accounting policy
    election that is consistent with
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    historical policy. As of January&#160;1, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> had
    recorded approximately $1.4&#160;million for the payment of
    tax-related interest and penalties including amounts recorded
    upon adoption of FIN&#160;48. For the year ended
    December&#160;31, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> recorded
    approximately $1.4&#160;million and $0.1&#160;million for the
    payment of tax-related interest and penalties, respectively. To
    the extent interest and penalties are not assessed with respect
    to uncertain tax positions, amounts accrued will be reduced and
    reflected as a reduction of the overall income tax provision.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    It is reasonably possible that within the next 12&#160;months,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> will see
    a decrease of approximately $2.2&#160;million in unrecognized
    tax benefits and a related $0.3&#160;million of interest and
    penalties a result of the expiration of various applicable
    statute of limitations and the settlement of various federal,
    state, and international audits.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

<TR>
    <TD width="5%"></TD>
    <TD width="95%"></TD>
</TR>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">10.&#160;&#160;</FONT></B>
</TD>
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Related-Party
    Transactions</FONT></B>
</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    In July 2007, one of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    subsidiaries purchased real property from entities that were
    owned by a
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> officer
    and an immediate relative of such officer. The purchase price of
    the property totaled approximately $3.6&#160;million. That
    subsidiary had previously leased such property for its
    operations. For the years ended December&#160;31, 2007, 2006 and
    2005, <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    paid $63,000, $108,000, and $108,000, respectively, for such
    annual lease costs.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    An additional
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    subsidiary leases its facilities from a company that is
    partially owned by a
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> officer.
    For the years ended December&#160;31, 2007, 2006 and 2005,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> paid the
    company approximately $378,000, $372,000 and $312,000,
    respectively for such annual lease costs.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <BR>
    F-21
</DIV><!-- END PAGE WIDTH -->

<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV style="width: 87%; margin-left: 6%"><!-- BEGIN PAGE WIDTH -->

<DIV style="margin-top: 18pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">W-H
    ENERGY SERVICES, INC.<BR>
    </FONT></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">NOTES&#160;TO
    CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL
    STATEMENTS&#160;&#151;&#160;(Continued)</FONT></B>
</DIV>
<DIV style="margin-top: 18pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    Chairman, President and Chief Executive Officer is the owner of
    a publishing company which made payments to
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> of
    approximately $14,000, $59,000 and $42,000 for the years ended
    December&#160;31, 2007, 2006 and 2005, respectively, primarily
    for rental of office space.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

<TR>
    <TD width="5%"></TD>
    <TD width="95%"></TD>
</TR>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">11.&#160;&#160;</FONT></B>
</TD>
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Shareholders&#146;
    Equity</FONT></B>
</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Stock
    options</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    On May&#160;10, 2006,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    shareholders approved
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    2006 Stock Awards Plan (the &#147;2006 Plan&#148;). Under the
    2006 Plan,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> may
    grant equity-based awards including stock options, stock
    appreciation rights, restricted stock, restricted stock units,
    performance units and other stock-based awards. An aggregate of
    1,100,000&#160;shares of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    common stock are reserved for issuance under the plan, subject
    to adjustments as described in the plan. The number of shares
    reserved for issuance will be reduced only by the number of
    shares of common stock delivered in payment or settlement of
    awards. Any shares of common stock issued in connection with
    restricted stock, restricted stock units, performance units or
    other full value awards will count against the limit of reserved
    shares as 1.7&#160;shares of common stock for every one share of
    common stock issued. Shares of common stock issued in connection
    with other types of awards will be counted against the limit of
    reserved shares as one share of common stock for every one share
    of common stock issued. Each award granted under the 2006 Plan
    is subject to such terms and conditions as the compensation
    committee of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    Board of Directors may approve.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    Under the 2006 Plan,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> is no
    longer permitted to issue new awards under its 1997 Stock Option
    Plan (the&#160;&#147;1997 Plan&#148;). As of December&#160;31,
    2007, options to purchase 1,220,138&#160;shares of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    common stock were outstanding under the 1997 Plan. The options
    currently outstanding under the 1997 Option Plan vest ratably
    over a four-year period, commencing on the grant date, in 25%
    increments after each year of service has been completed, and
    will expire ten years from their respective grant dates. The
    terms of each option also provide that if an optionee&#146;s
    employment terminates for any reason, the option may be
    exercised during the three-month period following such
    termination, but only to the extent vested at the time of such
    termination.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    On March&#160;29, 1999, prior to its initial public offering,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> issued
    options to purchase 900,900&#160;shares of
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    common stock to its chief executive officer under a separate
    non-statutory option plan. These options have a
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">10-year</FONT> term
    and an exercise price of $4.55 per share. As of
    December&#160;31, 2007, the remaining unexercised options to
    purchase 345,000&#160;shares of common stock were vested.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    As of December&#160;31, 2007, there was approximately
    $2.6&#160;million of total unrecognized compensation cost
    related to nonvested stock option awards that is expected to be
    recognized over a weighted-average period of 1.3&#160;years. The
    intrinsic value for stock options is defined as the difference
    between the current market value and the grant price. During the
    years ended December&#160;31, 2007 and 2006, cash received from
    options exercised was approximately $6.8&#160;million and
    $17.4&#160;million, respectively, and the excess tax benefit
    from share-based compensation totaled approximately
    $5.6&#160;million and $13.3&#160;million, respectively.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <BR>
    F-22
</DIV><!-- END PAGE WIDTH -->

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Restricted
    Stock</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    On February&#160;7, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> awarded
    a total of 52,000&#160;shares of restricted stock under its 2006
    Stock Awards Plan to certain employees, including
    20,000&#160;shares awarded to
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H&#146;s</FONT>
    Chairman, President and Chief Executive Officer. The fair value
    of the restricted stock issued was approximately
    $2.3&#160;million and is being recognized as compensation
    expense over the vesting periods ranging from 31&#160;months to
    48&#160;months.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    On May&#160;9, 2007,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> awarded
    a total of 15,000&#160;shares of restricted stock under its 2006
    Stock Awards Plan to its independent directors. The fair value
    of the restricted stock issued was approximately
    $0.9&#160;million and is being recognized as compensation
    expense over the 48&#160;month vesting period.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    For the years ended December&#160;31, 2007, 2006 and 2005,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    recognized $2.5&#160;million, $1.2&#160;million and
    $0.5&#160;million in compensation expense relating to restricted
    stock awards.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <BR>
    F-23
</DIV><!-- END PAGE WIDTH -->

    approximately $6.4&#160;million of total unrecognized
    compensation cost related to nonvested restricted stock awards
    that is expected to be recognized over a weighted-average period
    of 2.6&#160;years.

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

<TR>
    <TD width="5%"></TD>
    <TD width="95%"></TD>
</TR>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">12.&#160;&#160;</FONT></B>
</TD>
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">401(k)
    Plan</FONT></B>
</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    maintains a 401(k) plan that enables employees to defer up to
    specified percentages of their annual compensation and
    contribute such amount to the plan.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> may
    contribute a matching amount for each participant equal to a
    discretionary percentage determined annually by
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H.</FONT>
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> may also
    contribute additional amounts at its sole discretion.
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> matching
    contributions were approximately $7.2&#160;million,
    $4.8&#160;million and $2.7&#160;million for the years ended
    December&#160;31, 2007, 2006 and 2005, respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

<TR>
    <TD width="5%"></TD>
    <TD width="95%"></TD>
</TR>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">13.&#160;&#160;</FONT></B>
</TD>
    <TD>
    <B><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Operating
    Segments</FONT></B>
</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    Management has elected to aggregate its business unit segments
    based on the differences in each segment&#146;s customers, the
    products and services offered and other economic
    characteristics. Based on these requirements, management has
    identified the following reportable segments: (1)&#160;drilling
    related products and services and (2)&#160;completion and
    workover related products and services. The accounting policies
    of the operating segments are the same as those described in the
    summary of significant accounting policies.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Drilling
    Related Products and Services</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    The drilling segment provides products and services used by oil
    and natural gas companies, drilling contractors and other
    oilfield service companies for the drilling of oil and natural
    gas wells. These products and services are used primarily
    throughout North America and in select international areas. This
    segment includes the following business lines:
    (1)&#160;logging-while-drilling;
    (2)&#160;measurement-while-drilling; (3)&#160;directional
    drilling; (4)&#160;down-hole drilling motors; (5)&#160;drilling
    fluids and (6)&#160;rental tools.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Completion
    and Workover Related Products and Services</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    The completion and workover segment provides products and
    services primarily to customers onshore in the United States and
    offshore in the Gulf of Mexico. These products and services
    include: (1)&#160;cased-hole wireline logging, perforating,
    tubing conveyed perforating and associated rental equipment;
    (2)&#160;coiled tubing; (3)&#160;completion fluids and
    (4)&#160;rental tools.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 2%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: Arial, Helvetica; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">

    <B><I><FONT style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times">Summary
    Information</FONT></I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT>
    recognizes revenues, cost of revenues, selling, general and
    administrative expense, research and development expense and
    depreciation and amortization expense by segment. Interest
    expense and other income
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    <BR>
    F-24
</DIV><!-- END PAGE WIDTH -->

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    On February&#160;1, 2008,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> awarded
    a total of 140,500&#160;shares of restricted stock under the
    2006 Plan to certain executives and employees. The fair value of
    the restricted stock issued was approximately $7.9&#160;million
    and will be recognized as compensation expense over the vesting
    period of four years.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt; font-size: 1pt">&nbsp;</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; margin-right: 0%; text-indent: 4%; font-size: 10pt; font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times; color: #000000; background: #FFFFFF">
    On February&#160;22, 2008,
    <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">W-H</FONT> acquired
    a company that provides drilling jars and related parts to the
    oil and natural gas industry for cash consideration of
    approximately $47.5&#160;million.
</DIV>
